SECTION 6.1
DEFINITIONS AND DETERMINATION OF CG LOCATIONS

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
1 GENERAL
The purpose of this section is to collect together certain information
to be used in combination with the weights and centres of gravity manual,
in order to optimise loading and to determine the centre of gravity
location for an aircraft in service.
2 WEIGHTS AND CENTRES OF GRAVITY
2.1 Weights, conventional terms used:
2.1.1 Maximum Weight
This is the maximum approved take-off weight as defined in section 2
or the supplements to the manual. Under no circumstances should this
value be exceeded.
2.1.2 Empty Weight (EW)
The empty weight is the aircraft weight as defined by the aircraft- R
empty inventory. R
R
It includes, at least, the weight of the following components R
- Fixed balance weights (ballast) R
Unusable fuel R
Full quantity of operating fluids, i.e. R
· Oil R
· Hydraulic fluid R
· Other fluids required for the normal operation of the aircraft R
system. R
Indispensable equipment items, i.e. those which make the aircraft R
airworthy. R
Fixed equipment items (navigation instruments, radio-units, step R
ladder, fire extinguishers, cabin equipment, etc.) R
R
NOTE Prior to a weighing operation an inventory of the aircraft must R
be made (even briefly) as the empty weight can only be used if R
it is accurately defined. R
2.1.3 Operating Empty Weight (OEW) R
The OEW correspond to the total weight of the helicopter ready for a R
specific type of operation. The OEW includes neither usable fuel nor R
the payload. R
This is the sum of : R
- Empty weight (EW) R
- Weight of the crew with its baggage R
- Equipment required according to mission R
R
The OEW varies in accordance with the mission contemplated. R
2.1.4 Payload R
The payload corresponds to the total weight of passengers, baggage and R
freight including any non-revenue load. R
532 A2 6.1
06-34 Page 1
*01*


PDF PAGE 34
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
2.1.5 All Up Weight (AUW)
This is the sum of the OEW, the payload and fuel quantity. R
2.2 Centre of Gravity: Definition, Datums and Limitations (Fig. 1, 2 and 3)
2.2.1 Definition
The centre of gravity is defined by dimensions measured perpendicularly
to the three basic datum planes.
2.2.2 Datums
The datum trihedral is formed by the following planes
- A horizontal plane, the cabin floor datum, is the Z datum plane.
- A vertical plane perpendicular to the cabin floor datum. This Y
datum plane is the aircraft plane of symmetry.
Dimensions to the left (port) are known as negative, dimensions to
the right (starboard) as positive. The lateral reference stations
are given in Fig. 3.
- A vertical plane perpendicular to the two mentioned above, situated
183.86 in (4.67 m) forward of the centre of the main rotor. This is
the
X datum plane, from which the longitudinal reference stations
are measured. The longitudinal reference stations are given in
Fi g. 2.
The cabin floor datum is materialized by the surface of the cabin
floor.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
3 WEIGHING
Weighing is the only reliable way of obtaining:
- Operating Empty Weight (OEW),
- Empty aircraft centre of gravity (CG) location.
R
R
The aircraft must be weighed:
- On leaving the factory,
- Following any main modification.
R
R
4 CG COMPUTATION
NOTE: First, the load should be distributed as evenly as possible.
4.1 Method
The distance between the centre of gravity and the datum plane is found
by means of the following expression
Sum of moments = CG location
Sum of weights
- Determi ne the OEW. R
Refer to tables provided in Sections 6.2 and 6.3, then totalize weights
and moments.
- Check that the total weight is less than the maximum authorised
takeoff weight.
- Calculate the CG location.
- Check that CG falls within permissible limits.
4.2 Example
4.2.1 Longitudinal CG
Weight CG location Moment
- Empty Weight 12374 lb 184.62 in 2284488 in.lb R

     3.  Autopilot engagement/disengagement (AFCS)

     5.  ICS/Radio PTT switch (ICS RADIO)

         (according to aircraft)                                               R

     8.  Cyclic stick trim release (TRIM REL) 
     9.  Attitude modification - Autopilot manual ( beep) trim control 
         (STICK-TRIM)                                                           
     10. Not used (SPARE)

         (SPARE) Copilot's side

     7.  Collective lever trim release

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL 
3.8 ILS Indicator  (Fig. 11)
3.8.1 Localizer Scale
       (1) Aircraft Reference  : fixed symbol
           Parameter    : LOC alignment reference.
           Displayed    : Permanently (even when no t in ILS approach).
           Color        : Yellow.
           Note         : Disappears in case of com plete FDC failure when
                          ILS mode is not selected.
       (2) Deviation Scale
 : fixed symbol
           Displayed    : When ILS mode is selected .
           Color        : White.
       (3) LOC Index
 : moves horizontally
           Parameter    : LOC deviation.
           Displayed    : When ILS mode is selected  and an ILS signal is
                          received.
           Colors       : Green   : LOC mode couple d on AFCS,
                          Magenta : LOC mode not co upled.
       (4) Reminder
           LOC index appears as shown when full-sca le deviation is reached,
           and disappears in case of ILS signal fai lure.
       (5) Arrows  : fixed symbols
           Displayed    : When the LOC beam is cut,  the arrows indicate the
                          direction of LOC index (3 ) deviating from the inner
                          point on one side of the scale to the inner point
                          on the other side.
           Color        : Amber.
       (6) Reminder
           Arrows disappear when LOC index reappear s during the interval
           between the first two circles of the sca le (the nearest to the
           symbol) or when changing ILS frequency.
                                                                               R
                                                                               R
                                                                               R

3.8.2 Glide/Slope Scale                                                        R
       Same as LOC scale.                                                      R
NOTE : LOC and G/S Messages  : fixed symbols (Fig. 12 item 3)            R
              Displayed   : In case of LOC or Glide  Slope signal fault.        R
              Color       : Red.                                               R
3.8.3 ILS/MARKER Beacon  (Fig. 12)
       (1) MARKER Beacon Indicators  : fixed symbols
           Displayed    : As aircraft flies over ma rker beacons.
           Colors       : OM (outer marker)  :  blu e,
                          MM (middle marker) : ambe r,
                          IM (inner marker)  :  whi te.
           Note         : All three indications are  displayed sequentially
                          during the test sequence.
       (2) MARKER Sensitivity Indicator
 : fixed symbol
           Displayed    : According to sensitivity selection : LO or HI.
           Color        : White if ILS mode is sele cted.
           Note         : Amber LO message is displ ayed if ILS mode is not
                          selected.
                                                                               R
                                                                               R
                                                                               R
3.9 Reconfiguration Status Indicator
 (Fig. 12)
     (4) Reconfiguration Messages
         Indicate reconfigured system components.
         Displayed      : - According to SDRU selec tion :
                            . FDC  :   FDC 1 or FDC  2 message,
                            . PSU  :   PSU 1 or PSU  2 message,
                            . DCU  :   DCU 1 or DCU  2 message.
                          - According to AFCAU sele ction :
                            . AFCP 1 or AFCP 2 mess age.
         Color          : Amber.
         Note           : RA reconfiguration messag e appears in
                          radioaltimeter display zo ne.

HOVER INDICATOR FUNCTION (Fig. 55)
(8) Groundspeed Indicator: Moving scale
Displays aircraft groundspeed components along and across heading.
R
Colors - Brown.
NOTE - The scale disappears when Invalid Doppler data or
existence of a Doppler memory condition.
R
R
(9) Groundspeed Indicator
General view of groundspeed scale (in kt).
(10) Selected Groundspeed Marker
Center of marker reads selected groundspeed components.
Reminder
Permanently.
- Green if A.NAV selected on DCU, engaged and
coupled on AFCS computers and master half-
system.
- Blue in other configurations.
Flashes green/white or blue/white according to
whether AFCS is engaged or not while selected
groundspeed value is changed.
Disappears in the event of AFCS function loss.
Displayed
Colors
Crippled mode
(11) Display of Selected Groundspeed Marker Outside Zone Limits
Arrow Moves along inner edge of compass card, pointing
to selected groundspeed marker.
Variable values, indicate along and acrossDigital readouts
Colors
Reminder
Crippled mode
heading selected groundspeed components.
Same as selected groundspeed marker (10), and
when selected groundspeed marker and/or
groundspeed scale moves out of compass card.
Same as selected groundspeed marker (10).
Same as selected groundspeed marker (10).
disappears in the event of AFCS function loss.
Displayed
(12) Display of Selected Groundspeed Marker in Crippled Mode
Displayed : If groundspeed data lost.
NOTE - Groundspeed indicator (8) and selected groundspeed marker
(10) are cancelled.
- Cancelled in the event of AFCS function loss.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
3.4 Cyclic Stick and Collective Lever
3.4.1 Cyclic Stick (Fig. 27)
(1) AFCS Pushbutton Function: AP Disengagement. R
Note
Press to disengage AFCS basic stabilization
modes.
Same as STAB pushbutton on AFCP.
R
R
Utilization
(2) Higher-Order Mode Pushbutton Function: Disengage all higher-order R
modes. R
(3) TRIM REL Pushbutton Function: Release trim loads on cyclic axes.
(4) 4-Way TRIM Switch Function: Cyclic axis beep trim control (spring
return to center).
3.4.2 Collective Lever (Fig. 28)
(1) TRIM Switch Function Collective axis beep trim control (spring
return).
(2) TRIM REL Trigger Switch Function: Release trim loads on
collective axis.
In addition to releasing the trim loads:
- The TRIM REL trigger switch inhibits the FLY-UP
function.
- In TRANS DOWN mode, any actuation on the trigger
for 3 seconds will shift the mode to HT/HOVER.
(3) GIA Pushbutton Function: Activates GO-AROUND and TRANS UP modes.
Note
Utilization - Go-around by engaging lAS hold mode on pitch axis
and VIS hold mode on collective axis, for
GO-AROUND Mode.
- Upward transition from hover on pitch axis and
collective pitch channel, for TRANS UP mode.
(4) AP HYD Toggle Switch Function: Cuts off AP hydraulic power.

3 STARTING AND CRANKING (Fig.3)
(1) Starting Pushbutton on fuel flow control lever
Function
(2) Microswitch
Function
Supplies electric power to starter, to starting solenoid
valve and to ignition units.
Inhibits, engine lever in aft position:
- PWR 1 or 2 ( in case of major failure of the DECU, PWR
warning lights is not inhibited)
- OIL Pl or P2
- DIFF NG
R
R
R
R
R
(3) Main and Standby Fuel Flow Control Valves
Function : Mechanical regulation of fuel flow according to fuel flow
control lever position.
(4) Acceleration Controller Cam
Function Mechanically restrains the fuel governor metering unit
according to fuel flow control lever position (shutdown or
automatic starting).
(5) Digital Engine Control Unit (DECU)
Function Governs automatic starting via the servo-valve (according
to t4 and Ng parameters).
(6) Level Valve (calibrated at 2.2 bar (31.91 psi))
Supplies fuel to injection wheel during acceleration phase
(pressure above 2.2 bar (31.91 psi)).
(7) Manual Starting Valve
Function
Function : Actuated by pilot for manual starting in case of
servo-valve malfunction (or governor).
(8) Pushbutton (with built-in indicator light) on instrument panel
Function
S
Color
Condition
R.SET
Color
Condition
Resets starting circuit after detection of line
short-circuit condition, or after testing.
Green
Starter operating.
Yellow
Test or short-circuit in starter line.
(9) Guarded Pushbutton
Function : Engine cranking.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
6 GOVERNOR TWIN-ENGINE, SINGLE-ENGINE & TRAINING RATINGS (Fig. 6)
(1) Digital Engine Control Unit 1 (or 2)
Function: - Processes a gas generator rpm (Ng) setpoint signal
depending on the free turbine rpm (NF) to drive the
hydromechanical governing system (6).
Automatically limits maximum engine power to transient
twin-engine power rating.
(2) Power Loss Board
Function: - Receives Ng 1 and Ng 2 inputs.
- Analyses and indicates any significant mismatch (DIFF NG
light on caution advisory panel).
- Determines and indicates faulty engine (POWER 1 or 2)
light on caution advisory panel.
- Supplies a signal to the DECU overriding the transient
twin-engine power limit to the DEI HI rating.
(3) DEI 1 (or 2) warning on engine auxiliary control unit
Function: Indicates use of OEI HI rating on corresponding engine. A
maintenance operation is required in order to cancel the
warning light.
(4) TRAINING IDLE 1 (or 2) Switch (Engine Auxiliary Control Unit)
Function Selected engine reverts to inflight idle rating, regulated
by DECU.
- Other engine is limited to derated (-5 %) training power
by its DECU.
- Power Loss Board (2) detects DIFF NG and activates OEI mode
(derated to training limit).
- T light illuminates on instrument panel and IFDS display.
(5) OEI Pushbutton on collective lever grips
Function: Press once to select OEI LO rating following DIFF NG. R
signal. R
Press again to select OEI HI rating R
NOTE: OEI ratings may not be used more than 3 times.
(6) Hydromechanical Regulator
Function: Provides engine regulation relative to commands received
from the digital control unit DECU (1).

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
8.2 Power Loss (Fig. Il)
(1) PWR TEST Pushbutton (with built-in indicator light) on engine
auxiliary control unit.
Function Tests POWER LOSS circuit, resulting in illumination of.
- PWR C, DIFF NG, PWR 1 and PWR 2 lights at 0.25 s
intervals.
Green NORM light, for 3 s, if the test is OK.
Amber FAIL light, for 3 s, if the test is not OK.
- DEI HI lEOs on ß Ng indicators, and DEI/HI messages,
during the test.
NOTE In flight, the PWR test overrides the intermediate stops,
allowing engines to operate at DEI ratings.
(2) Power loss Board
Function Analyzes engine and rotor tachometer signals
· Ng 1 and 2,
· NF 1 and 2,
· NR
- Detects and indicates an Ng difference (illumination
of DIFF NG), a governor Failure or a drive shaft
Failure.
· PWR 1 light (6) illuminates if Ng 1 < Ng 2 and
NR < NR mini or Ng 1 > Ng 2 and NR > NR max.
· PWR 2 light (6) illuminates if Ng 1> Ng 2 and
NR < NR mini or Ng 1 < Ng 2 and NR > NR max.
- Runs a self-test routine which causes PWR C light (3)
to illuminate in case of failure. Manual test is then
required in order to reset the POWER lOSS Function.
(3) POWER.C light on instrument panel.
Color Yellow
Condition: Failure of Power loss board.
(4) GOV (lor 2) light on instrument panel
Color Yellow
Condition: - Flashes
R
- Constant illumination
Redundancy failure (power
supply, sensor or static
droop anomaly).
minor or sensor failure.
R
R
R
R
(5) Fuel Flow Control Contact
Function Inhibits POWER 1 (or 2) and DIFF NG lights when
fuel flow controls are at minimum idle detent.
(6) POWER 1 (or 2) Light on caution advisory panel.
Color Red
Condition: Engine power failure.
Faulty engine goes to and remains at approx. 85 % NG
rating.
NOTE Instrument panel red ALARM light flashes whenever a red
light illuminates on the caution advisory panel.

9 BLEED VALVE CONTROL AND MONITORING (Fig. 12)
(1) BLEED OFFSET Pushbutton with built-in indicator light, on instrument
panel.
Function Controls offset threshold position for opening and closing
of bleed valve.
Indicator light 1 (or 2)
Color Blue
Condition Computer acknowledges offset threshold.
(2) 95
% NG Logic Circuit
Function When bleed valve threshold is offset, uses Ng data to
control opening and closing of bleed valve and activates
the solenoid valve (3) :
PRE and POST TU 223 :
- Closes at 98
% Ng during acceleration.
- Opens at 95 % Ng during deceleration.
R
R
R
(3) Solenoid Valve
Function Inhibits bleed valve closing control system by venting to
air (offset threshold operation).
(4) Pressure Sensor
Function Compares P2 and PO pressure and regulates bleed valve
via bleed valve vent line (normal threshold).
R
R
PRE TU 223
- Closes at 85
% Ng.
- Opens at 81 % Ng.
R
R
R
POST TU 223 :
- Closes at 88 % Ng.
- Opens at 84 % Ng.
R
R
R
(5) Bleed Valve
Function Creates air leakage flow between axial compressor and
centrifugal compressor to prevent surging.
(6) Bleed Valve Flag
Function Indicates bleed valve position.
Color .. Black bleed valve CLOSED
. Yellow & green bleed valve OPEN.

1 FUEL TANKS
1.1 Installation Description (Fig. 1) R
Six crash resistant self-sealing flexible cells located in the lower
fuselage structure are arranged in two separate groups, each supplying
one engine. Two pumps allows fuel to be transferred from one group to the
other.
- LH Group :
· LH longitudinal fuel tank (IL) : feeder tank,
· rear transverse fuel tank (IT),
· aft fuel tank (5th tank).
- RH Group :
· RH longitudinal fuel tank (2L) : feeder tank,
· front transverse fuel tank (2T),
· forward fuel tank (7th tank).
532 A2 7.8
04-46 Page 3
*01*


PDF PAGE 400
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
/:wo
Fi g. 1
532 A2 7.8
97-50 Page 4
*01*


PDF PAGE 401
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
1.2 Supplementary Fuel Tanks
The following optional additional fuel tanks may be installed
- Central (6th) fuel tank;
- External sponson-mounted fuel tanks,
- Cabin-mounted jettisonable fuel tank,
- Ferry tanks.
The external sponson, jettisonable cabin and ferry tanks are covered
in detail in individual supplements.
1.2.1 Central Fuel Tank (6th Fuel Tank) (Fig. 1)
This removable tank is installed in the sling compartment.
It supplies the transverse tank (2T) in the RH group.
It includes a contents gauge connected to the RH group indicator.
A crash-resistant self-sealing 6th fuel tank is also available as
optional equipment.
1.2.2 External Sponson-Mounted Fuel Tanks (EXT) (Fig. 1)
These two fuel tanks are installed in the landing gear sponson
fairings.
Fuel flows by gravity into the main fuel tanks.
Two solenoid valves controlled from the fuel management panel allow
fuel to flow from the LH sponson tank into the LH longitudinal tank
(IT), and from the RH sponson tank into the RH longitudinal tank (2T).
Each sponson tank includes two contents gauge, a bleed valve and a R
filler spout.
These tanks are crash-resistant and self-sealing.

7.8

1.2.3 Jettisonable cabin-Mounted Fuel Tank (Fig. 2)
This fuel tank (1) is installed in the centre structure.
A pyrotechnique system is fitted for in-flight jettisoning.
It is equipped with a contents gauge.
A solenoid valve controlled from the fuel management panel and a
manually operated standby valve accessible below the cabin floor allow
fuel to be transferred by gravity into the LH fuel tank group.
This tank is self-sealing.
1.2.4 Ferry Tanks (Fig. 2)
The ferry tanks (2) are mounted in the cabin to increase the aircraft
range for ferry missions.
The complete installation includes five removable tanks, but from R
one to five tanks may be installed at any time. R
Fuel is transferred by gravity into the main fuel tanks by means of
a manually operated valve beneath each ferry tank.
The forward ferry tanks supply the front transverse tank (2T)
(RH group), and the aft ferry tanks supply the rear transverse
tank (IT) (LH group).

1.3 Fuel Tank Capacities (Figure 3)
The fuel tank capacities, in various units, are provided in Figure 3.
The weights have been calculated using an average fuel density d = 0.79.
The values have been rounded off to the nearest unit.
NOTE The quantities provided are theoretical.
Actual quantities can be significantly different due to aircraft
levelling and manufacturing tolerances.

2 ENGINE FUEL SUPPLY SYSTEMS (Fig. 4)
Two separate systems each supply one engine by means of two booster pumps
drawing fuel from a feeder tank. The feeder tanks are kept full as long
as the other fuel tanks still contain fuel.
The two systems are opposite handed, but otherwise identical.
Only the system related to the LH tank group and the transfer lines are
illustrated in Figure 4.
2.1 Description
(1) Fuel Shutoff Valve
Function
(2) Fuel Flowmeter
Function
(3) Check Valves
Function
(4) Bypass Valves
Function
Shuts off engine fuel supply when cockpit FUEL
SHUTOFF or CUTOUT controls are actuated.
Measures and transmit fuel consumption to the
navigation computer.
Prevent fuel flow reversal at various points in
the system.
Allow fuel to reach engine when fuel filter
is clogged.
(5) High-Level Sensors
Function
(6) Aft Fuel Tank
Function
Illuminate high fuel level indicator light on fuel
management panel when all tanks in group are full.
5th fuel tank supplying rear transverse tank (IT).
(7) Capacitance Gauges
Quantity
Function
1 per fuel tank.
Transmit fuel quantity to fuel management panel for
dig ita l dis play. 532 A2 7.8

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
3 FUEL SYSTEM CONTROL AND MONITORING PROVISIONS (Fig. 5)
3.1 Installation Description R
Standard control and monitoring provisions include
- Fuel management panel on the control console.
- Two fuel pressure indicators on the instrument panel.
- A FUEL warning light on the Caution Advisory Panel which illuminates
whenever a fuel warning condition is indicated on the fuel management
panel (pressure drop, filter clogging, low fuel level).
- Two fuel shutoff levers on the control quadrant, actuating the
engine fuel shutoff valves.
3.1.1 Fuel Management Panel
(1) Toggle Switches
Function : Booster pump ON/OFF switches.
(2) FILT Indicator Lights
Color Amber
Condition: Differential pressure switch (Item 16 Fig 4) detects
a pressure difference of 2.17 psi (150 mbar) or more.
(3) PRESS Indicator Lights
Color Amber
Condition Pressure switch (Item 19 Fig 4) detects a fuel
pressure below 2.17 psi (150 mbar).
(4) XFER Switchs
Function 3-position transfer pump switchs 1 and 2.
- Left Transfer from RH group into LH group.
- Center Off.
- Right Transfer from LH group into RH group.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
3 LEFT HAND HYDRAULIC SYSTEM (Figure 3)
(1) Main Hydraulic Unit consisting of
- one hydraulic reservoir containing approximately 9.5 l of fluid,
- monitoring, regulation and distribution equipment.
(2) Filter
Function: Filters the hydraulic fluid before it returns to the
reservoir. It is fitted with a by-pass valve, set at 2 bars
(29 psi) and a clogging indicator set at 1.5 bars (22 pSi).
(3) Hydraulic Pump driven from the MGB
Function: Supplies the system at a pressure between 170 and 175 bars
(2466 and 2538 psi) at a flow rate which varies according to
demand. It sucks the fluid from the reservoir (1) from the
2 l level.
(4) Pressure Indicator (refer to para. 4)
(5) Main Servocontrols
- The left hand system supplies the upper chambers.
(6) Ta i l Servocant ro l
- The left hand system supplies the left hand chamber.
(7) Autopilot Hydraulic Unit (refer to para. 9)
(8) Rescue Hoist (optional equipment)
(9) Rotor Brake (refer to para. 5)
(10) Utility Hydraulic Accumulator charged on the ground by the auxiliary
electric pump (Item 19, Figure 4)
Function: - assists the main hydraulic pump
- maintains wheel brake and rotor brake pressure when
aircraft is parked.
NOTE A direct reading pressure gauge located between the pilot and
copilot seats shows the accumulator pressure.
(11) Wheel Brakes (refer to para. 6)
(12) Landing Gear Operating Jacks (refer to para. 7)
2 main landing gear jacks,
1 nose landing gear jack.
(13) Normal
I Emergency Selector R
Function: on emergency, enables the landing gear to be lowered R
using the standby pump (14). R
(14) Standby Electric Pump
Function: Supplies the emergency landing gear lowering system at R
a pressure of between 60 and 125 bars (870 and 1740 psi). R
Operation: Select lever (13) to standby position.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
1 GENERAL (Fig. la and lb) R
The electric power generating systems supply the aircraft with AC power
(200 VAC 400 Hz 3-phase, 115 VAC and 26 VAC 400 Hz single-phase) and with
28 VDC power.
1.1 AC Power
Electric power is produced by two alternators driven by the main
gearbox. Each alternator (ALT lor ALT 2) supplies 200 VAC 400 Hz
3-phase power to a distribution busbar (BUS lor BUS 2). One phase of
each bus supplies 115 VAC 400 Hz power. A 115/26 V stepdown transformer
powered from BUS 1 supplies 26 VAC 400 Hz power.

PDF PAGE 477
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
1.2 DC Power
Two transformer-rectifier units (TRU 1 and TRU 2) supplied with 200 VAC
400 Hz 3-phase power from BUS 1 and BUS 2 deliver 28 VDC power.
TR 1 supplies the secondary bus (SEC), and TR 2 supplies the primary
or essential bus (ESS). In normal operation, these two buses are
coupled by a bus-tie contactor (BTC).
A 24 VDC battery (BAT) is coupled to the essential bus.
It is used to start the engines when a ground power unit is unavailable.
It also constitute the standby power source for critical systems in
the event of failure of the other aircraft power systems.
1.3 Redundancy
In the event of a power generator failure, the corresponding system is
automatically switched to the remaining power generator. Each power
generator is capable of covering the power requirements of both
aircraft systems, except battery.
1.4 Ground Power Unit
On the ground the aircraft may be supplied by a ground power unit (GPU).
The AC external power receptacle supplies both the AC and DC systems via
BUS 1. The DC external power receptacle supplies only the DC systems
via the secondary bus. Aircraft power sources have priority over
external power sources.
1.5 Standby Battery
A standby battery independent of the electric power generating system
constitutes the ultimate source of emergency power to vital circuits.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
2 200 VAC 400 Hz 3-PHASE POWER GENERATION (Fig. 2a and 2b) R
The aircraft includes two independent 3-phase AC power generating systems.
Each comprises a 40 kVA alternator driven at constant speed directly by
the MGB and supplying a distribution system via an AC master unit.
The AC master unit contains all the power generating system components
except for the alternators, the secondary single-phase distribution
busbars and the control and monitoring provisions.
Master unit functions include:
- alternator voltage regulation,
- source and circuit protection,
- source coupling to the power supply system,
power system reconfiguration to ensure flight safety in the event of
a source failure,
- testability of all circuits.
3 115 VAC AND 26 VACj400 Hz SINGLE-PHASE POWER GENERATION (Fig. 2a and 2b) R
3.1 115 VAC 400 Hz Single-Phase Power Generation
Single-phase 115 VAC 400 Hz power is obtained between one phase and
neutral in each power system.
Three single-phase lines (A, B and C) ensure primary power distribution
from the AC master unit.
3.2 26 VAC 400 Hz Single-Phase Power Generation
A 115/26 V stepdown transformer in the AC master unit, supplied by
power system 1 produces 26 VAC 400 Hz power for aircraft circuits.
532 A2
7.12
06-34 Page 5
*01*


PDF PAGE 480
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
4 AC POWER DISTRIBUTION (Fig. 2a and 2b) R
AC power is distributed by two independant primary busbars
bus 1 is supplied by alternator 1 or the GPU, and bus 2 by alternator 2 R
or the APU. R
Both busbars are located in the AC master unit. They are identical in
every respect.
In normal operation, the busbars supply:
- the 3-phase power consumers,
- the single-phase distribution busbars,
- the 200 VAC 400 Hz / 28 VDC transformer rectifiers,
- the 115/26 VAC 400 Hz transformer (system 1 only).
4.1 Coupling
Aircraft power sources are coupled to the distribution systems by
contactors :
- GlC 1 and GlC 2 for alternators 1 and 2,
- EPlC and GlC 3 for the GPU and APU,
- lC 1 and lC 2 for the busbars
(*),
- BTC 1 and BTC 2 for the transfer functions.
R
R
Coupling may occur automatically in the AC master unit, or may be
manually selected using control pushbuttons.
NOTE: Alternator coupling has priority over the GPU.
4.2 Reconfiguration
In case of total failure (or absence) of a system power source, the AC
master unit automatically reconfigures the systems by means of bus-tie
contactors BTC 1 and BTC 2.
4.3 Protection
The master unit protects the power systems against the following fault
conditions
- underfrequency (UF),
- overfrequency (OF) (only for the GPU),
- undervoltage (UV),
- overvoltage (OV),
- feeder differential current (OP),
- phase sequence reversal.
(*) PRE SB 24.00.09 R

7 ELECTRIC POWER CONTROL AND MONITORING (Fig. 5, 6, 7 and 8)
On the Caution Advisory Panel :
- a red BAT TO light illuminates when the battery temperature reaches
70°C.
- a red DC light illuminates in the even of failure of both transformer-
rectifier units.
- a yellow ELEC light illuminates whenever a yellow light is illuminated R
on the electrical system control panel.
(1) Transfer Indicator Light
Color : Green.
Condition :Transfer of one power system to the other (ALT 1 or ALT 2).
(2) ALT 1 and ALT 2 Pushbutton (with built-in indicator lights)
Function : Deenergize alternators and control line contactar
(GLC 1 or GLC 2).
Upper Indicator Light
Color Yellow.
Condition Pushbutton selected OFF, or
Pushbutton ON with alternator failure, or
POST SB 24.00.09, short-circuit on BUS 1 or BUS 2.
R
R
Lower Indicator Light
Color White.
Condition: Pushbutton selected OFF.
(3) SEC BUS and ESS BUS Indicator Lights
Color Yellow.
Condition Short-circuit or voltage fault on bus corresponding to
warning light.
(4) TRU 1 and TRU 2 Pushbuttons (with built-in indicator lights)
Function : Control contactors (TRUC 1 and TRUC 2).
Upper Indicator Light
Color Yellow.
Condition: Pushbutton selected OFF, or
Pushbutton selected ON with TRUC contactar open.
Lower Indicator Light
Color White.
Condition: Pushbutton selected OFF.
(5) 2-Position BUS-TIE Switch
Positions OPEN - NORM (NORM position locked)
Functions OPEN - NORM: Manual control of bus-tie contactar (BTC).

3.2 Operation (Fig. 3 and 4)
(1) LH Pitot head.
(2) Landing gear flight/ground logic circuit.
(3) RH Pitot head.
(4) Circuit continuity fault sensors.
(5) PITOT Indicator Light (Caution Advisory Panel).
Color
Condition
Yellow.
: Illumination of lights on pushbuttons (6), (7) or (8).
(6) RH PITOT Pushbutton (with built-in indicator lights).
(7) STATIC Pushbutton (with built-in indicator lights).
(8) LH PITOT Pushbutton (with built-in indicator lights).
3.2.1 In Flight (Fig. 3)
Each Pitot head is heated separately.
Note The RH Pitot head total pressure ports heating circuit is R
supplied by the aircraft battery in the event of the failure of R
both alternators or both TRU. R
In icing conditions, the static ports are not deiced in this R
configuration, and the selector (Figure 1 item 9) must be moved R
to the interior static port. R
3.2.2 On the Ground (Fig. 4)
A landing gear logic board shunts the total pressure port
heating resistors in both Pitot heads and the static port
heating resistors to reduce the current draw.
4 ELECTRICAL CIRCUIT-BREAKERS
The Pitot heating system is protected by circuit-breakers.
Refer to COCKPIT section of this Flight Manual for circuit-breaker
locations.

3 COCKPIT HEATING, VENTILATION AND DEMISTING : CONTROL AND
MONITORING (Fig. 3)
(1) AIR CONO light on caution advisory panel.
Color Yellow.
Condition : If any yellow light illuminates on control unit.
(2) Control Unit on overhead panel.
(3) Temperature
Function
Adjustment Potentiometer
Selects the setpoint value for the heating temperature.
The temperature regulation system includes
- a regulator unit,
two temperature probes (recirculation air and air
+
P2 bleed air),
- a P2 air inlet regulating valve.
(4) FAIL light
Color
Condition
Yellow.
Air + P2 air mixture temperature is above 90°C,
- Air leakage at duct junctions (temperature sensors),
- 11 Ng detection
(5) COLD/HOT 3-position switch
COLD - HOT 2 spring-loaded positions.
Function : Manual control of the regulating valve to control the
air mixture temperature.
(6) MANU/REG pushbutton (with built-in indicator light).
MANU
Color Green.
Condition Manual regulation, activated by using switch (5).
REG
Color
Condition
Yellow.
Air
+ P2 air mixture temperature is BO°C.
(Regulating system failure requiring switchover to manual
mode).
(7) 7-Position Mode Selector
(A) Fast Demisting of Windscreen and Lower Windows

at

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
7 AIR COOLING
7.1 General (Fig. 8)
The air cooling for the cockpit and cabin is provided by two air
cooling units:
. one unit located in the LH sponson for the cockpit,
. one unit located in the RH sponson for the cabin.
(1) Air Cooling Units
Function: Condense the coolant fluid in order to supply liquid R
to the evaporators. R
(2) Evaporators
Function: Draw in the warm air with an electric fan and cools R
the air by heat exchange.
The cool air is then distributed to the different
ventilation ducts.
7.2 Distribution of Cold Air
7.2.1 Cockpit Air Distribution
- Ventilation/Air Cooling
Function: Distributes the cold air through the cockpit ventilation
system.
- Ventilation/LAir Cooling
+ Demisting//Air Cooling
Function: Distributes the cold air to the winscreen and lower
window demisting system.
7.2.2 Cabin Air Distribution
- Ventilation/Air Cooling
Function: Distributes the cold air to the cabin ventilation ducts.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
1 FIRE DETECTION GENERAL (Fig. 1)
1.1 Function
The fire detection system is designed to provide aural and visual
warning to the crew of any excessive temperature rise in the engine or
MGB compartments.
1.2 Components
(1) One fire detection circuit in each engine bay.
(2) Two parallel fire detection circuits in the MGB compartment.
2 FIRE EXTINGUISHING GENERAL (Fig. 2)
2.1 Function
The fire extinguishing system is designed to concentrate fire
extinguishing agent inside the fire zone.
R
R
R
2.2 Components
(1) Fire extinguisher 2 (RH engine).
(2) Fire extinguisher 1 (LH engine).
(3) Engine 1 fire extinguisher diffuser.
(4) Engine 2 fire extinguisher diffuser.
NOTE Switching provisions allow each fire extinguisher to discharge
into either engine bay.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
5 ENGINE FIRE EXTINGUISHING SYSTEM (Fig. 5)
5.1 Fuel Shutoff Control
(1) Fuel Shutoff Control Lever (one per engine)
Actuating a fuel shutoff lever:
- closes the fuel shutoff valve (3),
- closes the engine bay ventilation flap (4). R
Simultaneously actuating both fuel shutoff levers isolates the
batteries (5) from the aircraft power generating system by tripping
the battery
+ and - contactors.
Only the emergency power-consuming circuits remain supplied by
the aircraft battery:
- engine fire extinguishing system (1ST and
2ND SHOT),
- emergency landing gear extension,
- cockpit utility light.
5.2 General Cut-out Control R
(6) General Cut-out Handles R
A handle pulled by the pilot or copilot, actuates both fuel shutoff
controls and:
- the fuel shutoff valves (3) are closed,
- the engine bay ventilation flaps (4) are closed, R
- the aircraft batteries are isolated from the power generating
system.
In addition, both alternators (2) are isolated from the electrical
system by a cam on the control quadrant.
NOTE After action on any cut-out handle, the following systems remain R
operat iana l : R
R
- the engine fire extinguishing systems, R
- the cockpit utility lights, R
- the emergency landing gear extension system, R
- emergency floatation gear (number 2 light illuminated, the three R
helium cylinders are working), R
- the consumers supplied from the emergency battery: R
· stand-by horizon, R
· emergency exit lighting system, R
· DC short-circuit protection system. R
- the NR self-contained. R

they may be mixed together only in case of necessity. In normal
use changing the hydraulic fluid requires the hydraulic system
to be drained completely and flushed.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL 
  
   
                                  SECTION 9.1
  
   
                      GUIDANCE FOR CARGO SLING OPER ATIONS
  
   
   
1  PERSONNEL TRAINING
  
   
   Cargo sling operations may only be conducted by pilots who already have  
   considerable experience with their aircraft.  
   
   No pilot may perform solo cargo-carrying flights   without first having  
   accomplished such operation in the company of an  instructor.  
   
   Mechanics on ground duty must be fully informed by the pilot before each  
   new operation, in particular as regards :  
   - their position on the ground considering the p roposed flight path ;  
   - the direction in which to move away ;  
   - the hook-up operation ;  
   - hand signals to be used or radio instructions ;  
   - protective equipment : helmets, gloves, glasse s (if applicable) ;  
   - the number of round trips between replenishmen ts ;  
   - the manner of retrieving slings and nets.  
   
   
2  IMPERATIVE PRE-OPERATIONAL CHECKS
  
   
2.1  Helicopter condition
  
   
     In addition to the usual examination of the he licopter, the release unit  
     must be carefully examined and the mechanism c hecked for correct release  
     operation.  
   
2.2  Condition of sling equipment
  
   
     The nets, strops and sling must be examined th oroughly. Any worn or  
     frayed components are to be discarded.  
   
     The cables, strops and shackles must be capabl e of carrying three times  
     the maximum anticipated load.  
   
2.3  Preparation of loads
  
   
     Check the weight of the loads and the method o f suspension.  
   
2.4  Condition of loading and unloading areas
  
   
     Remove or tie down all that might be displaced  by the rotor downwash.  
   
2.5  Total weight of helicopter with load
  
   
     Define maximum acceptable load compatible with  terrain configuration  
     and atmospheric conditions. Unless the platfor ms are in clear surroundings  
     and fairly large, consider as maximum weight t hat which can be held in  
     hover OGE in calm air over the higher of the t wo platforms (take-off  
     or landing).  
   
   
      
 
 532 A2 9.1
  
                   97-50 Page 1
 *01* 

PDF PAGE 602
   
 
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL 
  
   
3  AIRBORNE LOADS
  
   
   Heavy loads, such as bags of cement or drums of kerosene, which are carried  
   in a net, present no particular problem.  
   Special precautions must be taken in the case of  bulky loads, which have a  
   tendency to oscillate and even to "float" during  transport on the sling.  
   Permeability to air can have a stabilizing effec t on a bulky load : for  
   example, a cable car should be carried with both  its doors open.  
   Never carry an airfoil alone : there is a great risk of the airfoil flying  
   up into the tail rotor.  
   If several cables are used to sling the load, th ey must be long enough to  
   form an angle of less than 45° between cables at  the point of suspension  
   under the helicopter ; experience shows that osc illation of the load is  
   thus less likely to occur.  
   On the other hand, if the load is slung on a sin gle sling cable, it is  
   preferable that a fairly short cable be used as there is then less risk  
   of the load swinging, and it is easier to judge the height of the load  
   during approach.  
   For the retrieval of crashed helicopters it is g enerally possible to use  
   a lifting ring on the rotor shaft.  
   Airplanes are carried using straps passing under  the fuselage or under the  
   wings. The cables must be attached in such a way  that the airplane is  
   slightly nose down when the helicopter is hoveri ng.  
   
   
4  FLIGHT PRECAUTIONS
  
   
   After hooking on the load the ground mechanic is  to check the position of  
   sling cables then move away. The pilot must then  make sure that the mechanic  
   has moved away and has given the hand-signal for  takeoff.  
   Power must be applied slowly enough to allow the  helicopter to centre  
   itself above the load.  
   Take-off must be vertical to prevent dragging th e load along the ground or  
   striking any obstacle.  
                                                                               R      
   If the load starts to swing, reduce speed.  
   Approach must be made head into the wind with gr adual reduction in  
   airspeed, and transition into hover high enough above the ground to  
   eliminate the risk of dragging the load.  
   Set the load down, then reduce collective pitch sufficiently to slacken  
   the cables before opening the release unit hook ; this also allows the  
   pilot to ensure that the load is deposited. If t he cables are long enough,  
   move sideways a little before opening the hook, to prevent the slinding  
   items from falling onto the freight.  
   
   Even after the mechanic has signalled that the l oad is released, move  
   away as if it were not ; this is an advisible pr ecaution against possible  
   misinterpretation of signals.  
   
   Never fly away with an empty net or an unballast ed sling
.

                                 SECTION 9.2 
  
                KANNAD - 406 AP EMERGENCY LOCATOR T RANSMITTER 
 
 
1  GENERAL 
  
   The emergency locator transmitter radio beacon s ignals simultaneously on  
   the international distress frequencies 121.5 MHz , 243 MHz and 406.025 MHz  
   to aid search and rescue operations.  
   It can be actived manually or automatically in c ase of a crash. 
   The emergency locator is connected to the FMS/GP S, it will transmit the  
   last position of the aircraft before the crash. 
  
2  DESCRIPTION OPERATION 
  
   The KANNAD 406 AP beacons consist essentially of  the following : 
   - A transmitter  attached to the structure insid e the intermediate  
     rear RH cargo hold. 
   - An external antenna located in the upper secti on of the intermediate  
     structure. 
   - A remote control unit located on the instrumen t panel.

3  OPERATING PROCEDURE 
  
3.1  Pre-flight check 
  
      - In cockpit : check that remote control swit ch is set to ARMED 
  
      - On Transmitter : Check that ARM-OFF-ON swit ch is set to ARM. 
  
3.2  Operation Testing 
  
     The self-test mode is a temporary mode. 
  
     This mode is selected either : 
  
      - When switching from OFF to ARM the switch o f the ELT. 
  
      - When switching to RESET & TEST on the remot e control panel (provided 
        that the switch of the ELT is in position A RM). 
  
     The buzzer operates during the self-test proce dure. 
  
     NOTE : It is strictly prohibited to test the E LT by transmitting. 
  
3.3  Post-flight check 
  
     After landing, set the VHF receiver to 121.5 M Hz to ensure that the 
     emergency locator transmitter has not accident ally been switched ON. 
  
  
4  OPERATING PROCEDURE 
  
4 1  Automatic operation 
  
     The transmitter is actuated automatically in t he event of an impact 
     assuming the switches are set to ARMED. 
  
     NOTE : The RESET & TEST position stops locator  transmitting and resets  
            the impact detector  
  
4.2  Manual operation 
  
     The unit may be actuated manually by setting t he ELT control switch to 
     ON or, the switch on transmitter being set to "ARM" by setting the switch 
     on the remote control panel to "ON". 
  
4.3  Portable Operation 
  
     The transmitter may be used for self-contained  operation on the ground 
     as follows : 
  
     - Remove the transmitter from its mounting bra cket. 
     - Disconnect the coax from the aircraft antenn a. 
     - Select an unobstructed area. 
     - Extend the built-in tape antenna. 
     - Place the unit upright with the antenna on t op. 
     - Switch on the transmitter by setting the ARM -OFF-ON switch to ON. 
     - The transmitter output signal should be audi ble in the headphones. 
     - Set switch back to ARM or AUTO.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
3 CONTROL AND MONITORING (Figure 1)
The system is controlled and monitored using the following items
A control unit, which includes
· a three-position REF-OFF-DEF switch (10) which controls the
refueling or defueling operation; this switch is locked in the OFF
position.
five lights which illuminate to indicate opening of the pressure
refuelling solenoid valves of the corresponding tanks: INT (4)
internal tanks, CAB (2) jettisonable cabin tank, EXT (3) sponson
external tanks.
· a numerical displaying the programmed fuel quantity to refuel R
or defuel (7), controlled via an INCR-DECR (8) selector. R
a TEST button (6) used to check opening of the solenoid valves via the
fuel management panel. The LEDs (1) illuminate to indicate that the
control circuits are operating correctly.
· a two-position FIRST refuelling or defuelling priority selector (9)
INT-CAB.
· a label (5) indicating the units of measurement.
- The control providing the automatic opening and closing of the solenoid
valves in the basic aircraft system, which includes:
· the fuel contents gauging system,
· the high level contactors, for emergency purposes.
4 OPERATING PROCEDURE
The operating procedure for the pressure refueling and defueling
installation is given in section 8.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
SECTION 9.79
ELECTRIC MULTIPURPOSE AIR INTAKES
1 GENERAL
1.1 Function
The ELECTRIC MULTIPURPOSE AIR INTAKE system protects the engines
against:
- Engine air intake icing,
Ingestion of snow or ice,
- Ingestion of sand or dust.
This system has two modes of operation
with bullets open
. Direct supply of ram air,
with bullets closed (flight in icing conditions or snow or hailstone R
or sand-laden atmosphere) R
. Air is supplied through filter boxes.
1.2 Electrical power supply
The system is supplied with 28 Vdc from the aircraft power system via the
primary and secondary busbars.
The locations of the system protection circuit breakers are given in
Section 7.2.
2 DESCRIPTION (Figure 1)
The system consists of
a stub-frame, attached to the engine cowlings, fitted with two modules
(1) and two sand extractor fans (2),
- two modules, installed along the engine centreline, each including:
· an air intake which can be closed by a mobile bullet driven axially by
an electric actuator,
· seven filter boxes equipped with sand particle separators,
- a P2 air supply system to pressurize the seals,
- control and monitoring unit.
- an electrical system to supply:
· bullet control actuators,
· sand extractor fans,
· system controls and indicators,
532 A2
9.79
04-46 Page 1
*01*


PDF PAGE 706
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL
3 CONTROLS AND INDICATORS (Figure 1)
Controls and indicators enable the system to be operated in anti-icing or
sand filter configuration (bullets closed).
3.1 Anti-icing configuration
The anti-icing controls and indicators are:
- two 2-position ANTI-ICING switches (9) (RH engine) and (11)
(LH engine) :
. OFF: Opens the bullet,
. ON : Closes the bullet,
- two green CLOSED - LH BULLET (4) and CLOSED - RH BULLET (7) lights which
come on two indicate that the relevant bullet is locked in closed
position,
- two amber FAIL - RH BULLET
(8) and FAIL - LH BULLET (12) lights which come
on to indicate a malfunction of the relevant bullet.
3.2 Sand filter configuration
The sand filter controls and indicators are:
- a three-position SAND FILTERS selector switch (10) :
OFF Opens the bullets,
CONTINUOUS Closes the bullets and switches on the extractor fans,
AUTO L/G If landing gear down, closes the bullets and switches
on the extractor fans via the landing gear strut micro-
switch,
- If landing gear up, opens the bullets and switches off
the extractor fans via the landing gear strut micro-
switch,
- two green CLOSED - LH BULLET (4) and CLOSED - RH BULLET (7) lights which
come on to indicate that the relevant bullet is locked in closed
position,
- two amber FAIL - RH BULLET
(8) and FAIL - lH BULLET (12) lights which
come on to indicate a malfunction of the relevant bullet,
- two amber FAN FAIL lights (5) and (6) which come on to indicate a
malfunction of the relevant extractor fan,
NOTE The automatic and manual sand filter controls are inoperative when
one or both of the anti-icing controls are in ON position.

 *01*
 
 
                                   SECTION 9.91 
 
                    I - CHLIO S FLIR INFRA-RED IMAG ING SYSTEM                  R 
 
 
A COMPLETE DESCRIPTION OF SYSTEM OPERATION IS GIVEN  IN MANUFACTURER'S           
PILOT GUIDE.       
 
 
1  GENERAL 
 
1.1  Purpose 
 
     The CHLIO FLIR installation enables surveillan ce, navigation, 
     reconnaissance detection and identification mi ssions to be flown.          
     The system provides stabilized pictures from t he infra-red camera.         
 
1.2  Characteristics 
 
     Angular displacement : 
     - elevation : +40°/-90° 
     - bearing : +180°/-180° 
 
     Optical characteristics : 
     In wide-angle mode (WA) : 
     - Horizontal field of view : 24° 
     - Vertical field of view   : 16° 
 
     In narrow field mode (NF) : 
     - Horizontal field of view : 3°                                            
     - Vertical field of view   : 2.25°                                        
 
     As an electro-optical telescope (EOT) : 
     In wide angle mode (WA + EOT) : 
     - Horizontal field of view : 12° 
     - Vertical field of view   : 8° 
 
     In narrow field mode (NF + EOT) : 
     - Horizontal field of view : 1.5°                                          
     - Vertical field of view   : 1.125°.                                       

PDF PAGE 722
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 9.91 
 
                        97-50 Page 2
 *01*
 
 
     Focusing : 
 
     The focusing control enables the best focusing  adjustment to be  
     selected for the following distance ranges : 
 
     - in wide angle mode : 
       from infinity to 5.2 m (typical value) 
 
     - in narrow field mode : 
       from infinity to 191 m (typical value). 
 
     Sensor cooling time : 
 
     Under nominal power supply conditions (28 V) a nd with a stable outside  
     air temperature of 20°C, the time to cool the sensor is less than or  
     equal to 9 minutes (typical value 5 min.). 
     Within the operating temperature range, the ma ximum cooling time is  
     provided by the following table : 
 
                                                                      
   |                            |                  |                 | 
   |   Outside air temperature  |      - 40°C      |      + 70°C     | 
   |                            |                  |                 | 
   |                            |                  |                 | 
   |                            |                  |                 | 
   |   Time to cool             |    <  7.5 min.   |      11 min.    | 
   |                            |                  |                 | 
   |                            |                  |                 | 
 
1.3  Power Supply 
 
     The installation is powered by the 28V dc airc raft power supply. 
     The locations of the installation protection c ircuit breakers are  
     provided in section 7.2. 
 
2  DESCRIPTION - OPERATION 
 
   The installation consists of : 
   - A control grip located in front of the inter-s eat console. 
   - A sensor control unit located on the inter-sea t console. 
   - A twin focal length IR turret located beneath the radome. 
   - A camera electronic control unit. 
   - A turret electronic control unit. 
   - A control unit located on the inter-seat conso le enables the video  
     contrast to be adjusted on the NMD-configured displays.

NOTE With NORM + GAIN selected, the push-button is used to adjust the
gain by operating it UP/Down. Pressing to the right generates
a zero gain picture and inhibits the telescope function (and the
display is modified). Pressing again on the right reverts to
normal operation.

                                 SECTION 9.91 
                           
                        II - STAR SAFIRE 380-HD SYS TEM 
 
 
A COMPLETE DESCRIPTION OF SYSTEM OPERATION IS GIVEN  IN MANUFACTURER'S PILOT  
GUIDE.  
 
NOTE : Refer to SUP.49 (STAR SAFIRE 380-HD SYSTEM)f or the usage restrictions.      
 
1  GENERAL 
 
1.1  Purpose 
 
     The Electro Optical System (EOS) is an observa tion system based on an 
     Infra-Red (IR) camera and a day TV camera (HDE O) and a low light camera 
     (HDLL), with full high definition imaging for a wide range of critical  
     night and day missions.  
        
 
1.2  Characteristics 
 
     Angular displacement : 
     - elevation : +30°/-120° 
     - azimuth : +180°/-180° 
      
     Operational temperature : 
     - -40°C/+55°C 
 
     IR sensor fields of view:                                                 R 
     Three horizontal FOV : 30°, 4.9°, 0.9° + addit ional electronic zoom x2.   R 
                                                                               R 
     HD TV sensor fields of view:                                              R 
     Five optical FOV : 30°, 4.8°, 0.9°, 0.5° and 0 .25°.                       R 
                                                                               R 
     LL TV sensor :                                                            R 
     FOV continuous zoom : from 55° to 3.2° - x18,                             R 
                           from 60° to 2.2° - x30.                             R 
 
1.3  Power supply 
     
     The installation is powered by 28Vdc aircraft power supply. 
     The locations of the installation protection c ircuit breakers are  
     provided in section 7.2.      
      
 
1.4  System functionality                                                      R 
      
     Automatic Video Tracker (AVT) : The Automatic Video Tracker (AVT)         R 
     provides the capability to lock onto and track  target images within       R 
     the field of view. The AVT is capable of track ing a target with           R 
     either IR or TV video.                                                    R 
                                                                               R 
     3D GEO point mode :  When Geo-Point is used wi th the optional LRF Laser   R 
     Range Finder, the 3D Geo-Point mode provides a utomatic tracking of        R 
     non-geographic points such as man-made buildin gs, rooftops or other       R 
     elevated structures.                                                      R 
 
                                                                               R  

PDF PAGE 740
COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 9.91 
 
                          17-08 Page 10
 *01* 
 
 
1.4  System functionality (cont'd)                                             R 
 
     GEO rate mode : The Geo-Rate mode provides aut omatic Line-of-Sight (LoS)  R 
     steering for moving targets such as vehicles t ravelling on roads or       R 
     ships in waterways.                                                       R 
                                                                               R 
     Auto Scan : With AVT installed, and the autolo ck enabled, the system      R 
     will search a selected scan pattern area for a n unknown target then       R 
     automatically acquire and track an object that  satisfies the tracker      R 
     detection criteria.                                                       R 
                                                                               R 
     Track mode : The Track operating mode provides  automatic LoS steering     R 
     for moving targets. When the Track mode is eng aged, the auto-tracker      R 
     will lock onto and follow a target that satisf ies the tracker target      R 
     detection and track criteria. The AVT is capab le of tracking targets      R 
     using any active or manually selected sensor i nput (IR or EO).            R 
                                                                               R 
     Cage mode : Cage commands the TFU to a particu lar pointing angle with     R 
     respect to the vehicle while stabilizing the L oS against changes in       R 
     platform orientation. The system will Cage to the default (cage)          R 
     position when the system is powered on. Defaul t and additional Cage       R 
     positions are also available and programmable.                             R 
                                                                               R 
     Stow mode : Stow mode will nominally position the TFU in                  R 
     the -265° elevation, 180° azimuth position to protect the optical         R 
     windows. Stow position can be changed in the m enus.                       R 
                                                                               R 
     Inertial point : In Inertial Point mode, the T FU LoS will be stabilized   R 
     with respect to an inertial frame of reference .                           R 
                                                                               R 
     Heading Hold : In Heading Hold mode, the TFU L oS will maintain at a       R 
     constant pointing angle to the platform and wi ll not be compensated       R 
     for platform motion.                                                      R 
 
 
2  DESCRIPTION 
 
   The installation consists of : 
   - A gyro-stabilized Turret-FLIR Unit (TFU). 
   - A Universal Hand Control UNIT (UHCU). 
   - A Laser Interlock Unit (LIU). 
   - A BARCO MDU 268 Display. 
   - A AVALEX AVR8414 video recorder unit. 
   - A DLC50N Downlink system.

2.1  Synoptic 
                                    Figure 1 
2.2  Turret-FLIR unit (TFU) 
     The TFU consists of a gyro-stabilized multi se nsor imaging system with  
     all electronics integrated.            
     It contains a standard Mid-Wave Infrared (MWIR ) sensor, a HD TV sensor 
     (CCD RGB color) and a HD low light sensor (CCD  RGB color). 
     The TFU is capable of pointing the payload ove r the full 360° range of  
     azimuth continuously and from +30° to -120 in elevation. Gyroscopes in 
     the payload stabilize the TFU to dampen platfo rm-induced vibration and 
     motion, resulting in a smooth and steady image , even in the narrowest 
     Field-of-View (FOV).  
                                    Figure 2

2.3  Universal Hand Control Unit (UHCU) 
 
     The Hand Control Unit is used to power ON/OFF the TFU, command all  
     turret motors, sensors and laser features if a ny, enter the OSD Menu 
     and modify configurations.

2.3.1 FLIR contrast control units 
 
      The FLIR contrast control units, located on t he instrument panel  
      (Section 7.2, Figure 1), enables the NMD disp lay contrast to be adjusted 
      on pilot's and copilot's side.

*01* 
2.4 Laser Interlock Unit (LIU) 
    The LIU is used to manage the arming and firing  functions of the laser 
    pointer. By turning the key (2) from OFF to ARM , the light ARM/ON (1) comes 
    on, then the user can fire the laser, but it is  also necessary for complete 
    the logic to have the Weight-on-wheels logic mu st be in "flight" status and 
    not "ground" status. This second safety feature  ensures that non eye-safe  
    laser cannot be fired on the ground.

2.7  AVALEX AVR 8414 video recorder unit 
     The video-recorder is able to record and playb ack EOS video (Video in 
     control : VIC) including audio at the same tim e. 
     The video-recorder is equipped with one intern al and three external  
     storage options (Figure 3). The internal devic e has 54 GB of usable 
     memory. It has an external Universal Serial Bu s (USB) (11), a Secure  
     Digital (SD) (22) card and a Solid State Drive  (SSD) slot (23). 
     To access the USB port (11), remove the rubber  dust plug on the front  
     panel. To access the SD card and SSD slots, sl ide the latch (19) to the  
     left to release the display panel facia (Figur e 3). 
     The pause/play (4), Fast Forward (5) and Rewin d (3) buttons are disabled 
     while in record mode, except if using playback . 
     Do not remove a storage device while the Write  Status LED (21) is  
     blinking because data/video and the storage de vice may be compromised. 
     The video recorder is powered on, when the ON/ OFF switch of UHCU is on 
     (Refer to paragraph 2.3).

2.8  DLC50N Downlink system 
     The DLC50N controls the downlink of the EOS VI C in high definition and in 
     standard definition video from aircraft to a g round receiver over Line of 
     Sight (LOS), in the microwave frequency band ( primarily between 2.3 and 
     2.4 GHz). 
     The user interface on the DLC50N allows the us er to navigate the menu 
     system and monitor system status. The user int erface consists of four 
     pushbutton keys, a dual rotary knob (with inte gral push button), a  
     3-line display and various LEDs.

                                  SECTION 9.92 
                              CENTRAL CLEAR SYSTEM  
1  GENERAL 
   The central clear system is used to erase confid ential data's in  
   sub-systems and self-protection systems. 
   - Centralized radio control system.  
   - Crypto module KS-100. 
   - Electronic Warfare System (EWS). 
2  DESCRIPTION 
   The central clear unit is located on the overhea d panel. 
                                   Figure 1 
|        |                           |                                       | 
|  ITEM  |      DESCRIPTION          |                 FUNCTION              | 
|        |                           |                                       | 
|        |                           |                                       | 
|   1    | CENTRAL CLEAR             | NORM. : Norm al operation.             | 
|        | Guarded switch            |                                       | 
|        |                           | ERASE : Eras e confidential data's in  | 
|        |                           |         sub- systems radio,            | 
|        |                           |         self -protection systems.      | 
|        |                           |                                       | 
3  OPERATION 
   Activation of the guarded switch to ERASE positi on (Figure 1, item 1) 
   immediately erases all data stored in systems co nnected to it. 
   CAUTION : ACTIVATION OF THE CENTRAL CLEAR INSTAN TLY RESETS ALL ACTIVE AND 
             PRESET FREQUENCIES IN THE CENTRALIZED RADIO CONTROL SYSTEM.

 *01*
 
                                     SECTION 9.96 
 
                          I -  ELIPS DECOY DISPENSE R SYSTEM                    R 
 
                                                                              
       |                                                                     | 
       |                             IMPORTANT NOTE                           | 
       |                                                                     | 
       | FLYING WITH AN EMPTY CHASSIS IS PROHIBITED . BEFORE ANY FLIGHT, FIT  | 
       | THE DECOY DISPENSER WITH DUMMY MAGAZINE AS SEMBLIES, TRUE MAGAZINES  | 
       | LOADED WITH (LIVE OR INERT) CARTRIDGES, OR  BLANKING PLATES          | 
       |                                                                     | 
 
1  GENERAL 
 
   WARNING: CARTRIDGES (CHAFF OR FLARE) EXPLOSION O R DEPARTURE CANNOT BE        
            EXCLUDED IN CASE OF LIGHTNING STRIKE ON  AIRCRAFT. DELIBERATE        
            PENETRATION INTO METEOROLOGICAL CONDITI ONS WITH LIGHTNING RISK      
            SHALL BE AVOIDED WITH LIVE CARTRIDGES O NBOARD.                      
1.1  Purpose 
 
     The ELIPS decoy dispenser system is a passive aircraft protection system 
     which carries and fires infrared (IR) type M20 6 and electromagnetic (EM)   
     type RR170, Stanag 3821 Ed 1 conformable decoy  cartridges which are        
     stored in magazines.                                                       
 
1.2  Brief description of the system 
 
     The decoy dispenser system is shown in the blo ck diagram (Figure 4) and 
     consists of : 
     - A master arming safety switch (MASS), 
     - A control unit, 
     - A decoy dispenser selection and firing contr ol panel, 
     - A management and distribution unit (MDU), 
     - A firing override control on each cyclic sti ck grip 
     - Two decoy dispensers which can accommodate a  24-cartridge magazine, 
     - A link to the Radar warning Receiver. 
     In addition to the aircraft installation, the system includes : 
     - A rearming unit 
     - A data insertion module reader. 
 
1.3  Power supply 
 
     The system is supplied with 28 Vdc from the ai rcraft power system. 
     For safety purposes, the management and distri bution unit is powered 
     via the master arming safety switch (MASS). 
 
1.4  Protection 
 
     The system is protected by 2 circuit breakers.  Their locations are given 
     in Section 7.2. 
 
     The management and distribution unit is protec ted by a fuse accessible 
     from a side of the unit. 
 
1.5  Lighting 
 
     Is controlled using the lighting controls on t he strip on the overhead 
     panel and on the caution advisory panel.

                                 SECTION 9.96 
 
                    II - ELECTRONIC WARFARE SYSTEM (EWS) 
 
1  GENERAL 
 
   1.1 Characteristics  
 
       The Electronic Warfare System (EWS) is a sel f-protection system. 
       It provides to the aircrew essential situati onal awareness of the 
       surrounding electromagnetic environment. 
       It consists of a Multi Sensor Warning System  (MSWS) which is  
       fully integrated with a Chaff and Flare Disp ensers System (CFDS). It  
       also has a fully integrated Electronic Warfa re Controller (EWC), which 
       interfaces to a dedicated threat display (Th reat Warning Indicator 
       (TWI)). 
 
       The Electronic Warfare System provides the f ollowing functions : 
 
       - Missile warning, 
       - Laser warning, 
       - Radar warning, 
       - Chaff and flare dispensers, 
       - Display and control of the system. 
 
       NOTE : Refer to paragraph 3 for acronym's me aning. 
 
    1.2 Multi Sensors Warning System (MSWS)  
 
        The MWSS contains the following sub-systems , to detect and identify 
        threats : 
 
        - Missile Warning Sub-System  
          The Missile Warning Sub-system (MWSS) fun ction provides warning and  
          direction of arrival of Ultraviolet (UV) emissions from an  
          approaching missile's propulsion plume. 
          The MWSS consist of four Missile Approach  Warning Sensors (MAWS) and 
          a MWSS Controller Card. 
 
        - Laser Warning Sub-System 
          The Laser warning sub-system (LWSS) provi des for detection,  
          identification, tracking and direction fi nding of laser emissions 
          from laser sources such as Laser-guided m issiles (Beam Riders),  
          rangefinders, target designators and dazz lers. 
          The LWSS consist of Laser Warning Sensor (LWS) and LWSS Controller  
          Card.  
     
        - Radar warning Sub-System 
          The radar warning sub-system (RWSS) is ca pable of intercepting and  
          analyzing radar signals in the 0.7 GHz to  40 GHz frequency band and 
          including : 
          . Search 
          . Tracking 
          . Acquisition 
          . Guidance signal 
          The RWSS consist of two Dual Front End Re ceiver (DFER), four Spiral 
          Antenna Module (SAM) and three RWSS Contr oller Cards in the EWC.

1.3 Sensors and amplifiers 
    The EWS comprises the following equipment: 
    - 2 DFER (Dual Front End Receiver), 
    - 4 LWS (Laser Warning Sensor), 
    - 4 MAWS (Missile Approach Warning Sensor), 
    - 4 SAM (Spiral Antenna Module),

       5 : Safety Pin Unit (SPU)

     All components of this reactive system is pres ented in paragraph 2.8  
     up to paragraph 2.11.

     Refer to section 7.2 for circuit breakers loca tion.

2  DESCRIPTION  
   The Electronic Warfare System comprises : 
   - A Electronic Warfare Controller (EWC), located  in the avionics bay (at the 
     aircraft rear part), 
   - A Chaff and flare dispensing control panel (CF DCP), located on the  
     pedestal, 
   - An EWS control panel, located on the pedestal,  
   - A Threat Warning Indicator (TWI), located on t he bottom part of the 
     instrument panel, 
   - A EWS audio control panel located just below t he TWI, 
   - A Data Insertion Device (DID) located on the 3 rd crew member's panel and a 
     Data Maintenance Terminal (DMT),  
   - Chaff and Flare Dispensers (CFD) and Magazines  (MAG) located on each side 
     of the tail boom :  
     . 2 BOP-L31 pyrotechnical dispensers and 2 BOP -L31 MAG magazines. 
     . The dispensers have jettisoning capabilities . 
     . The cartridge types are: 
       - Chaff type 1 RR-170 cartridges using BBU-3 5/B squibs, 
       - Flare type 1 M206 cartridges using BBU-35/ B squibs, 
   - A Safety Pin Unit (SPU) with safety pin, locat ed at the bottom side of the 
     tail boom. 
2.1 System architecture 
                                   Figure 2                                     
                                                                               N

2.2 Electronic Warfare Controller (EWC)                             
    The EWC is the core of the EWS. I t receives and processes the signal 
    from the sensors and makes all the calculations  responsible to activate 
    the proper countermeasures. 
    The EWC functions are as follows : 
    - Radar warning analysis, 
    - Laser warning analysis, 
    - Missile approach warning analysis, 
    - Chaff and flare dispensing system management,  
    - EWC function includes the following : 
      . System management function, 
      . Threat response function, 
      . Data management, 
      . Dispensing actuation. 
    Three discrete blanking interfaces are provided  by the EWS for 
    electromagnetic compatibility with the onboard radio frequency 
    transmitters. When an inhibit signal is receive d from the transmitter,  
    EW C inhibits the signal reception on the corresponding  band. 
 2.3  EWS control panel 
     The EWS control panel centralizes functions su ch as : 
     - System ON/OFF, 
     - Preventive firing, 
     - Cabin firing authorization.

       - Platform (PLT) variant.

       The following mission status indicators are permanently displayed  
       on the screen : 
 
       - RWR : Status of radar warning function, 
       - MWS : Status of missile warning function, 
       - LWS :Status of laser warning function, 
       - C/F : Status of chaff and flare dispensing  function, 
       - UDFS : Status of UDFS function and its ver sion (x.x), 
       - PLT : Platform variant function with the p latform number (xx) 
               as received from the platform. If no  information is received, 
               a -- will be displayed, 
       - RWR CHN : Status of the sub-system of the radar warning function, 
       - MWS CHN : Status of the sub-system of the missile warning function, 
       - LWS CHN : Status of the sub-system of the laser warning function, 
       - C/F DSP : Status of the sub-system of the chaff and flare dispensing 
                   function, 
        
2.4.2  Threat warning indicators 
 
       The following mission status indicators are variable and are displayed 
       when necessary : 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
                                    Figure 5 
 
       - HIGH TEMP : Temperature condition of the E WC 300, 
       - 28VDC : Power supply condition of the airc raft, 
       - FL (Front Left) / AL (Aft Left) / AR (Aft Right) / FR (Front Right) :  
         Represents each quadrant of each warning f unction respectively, 
       - MAGID : A CFD magazine code condition, 
       - INVENTOR : Payload inventory, 
       - MISFIRE : Misfire condition, 
       - LH / RH : Represents each side of the disp ensing function, 
       - F-TRIG : CFDS is semi-automatic mode (yell ow when active). 
       - Safety pin : Safety pin information (inser ted or not). 
 
       The following colors represents the mission health status of the  
       associated function: 
       Green  : Only applicable to permanently disp layed status indications 
                Sub-system is fully functional. 
       Yellow : Sub-system is degraded. 
       Red    : Sub-system has a critical failure.

2.5  Loading/downloading interfaces: DTU/DID 
     The system needs to be configured by uploading  the configuration of the 
     system and the library named User Defined File  Subset (UDFS). 
     The flash memory card DID allows loading or ex tracting data. 
     The system supports recording of mission and m aintenance data. 
     DTU permit to load libraries (UDFS) and to unl oad flight recording files. 
     These data can be erased via the central clear  switch. 
2.6  EWS security ERASE 
     Refer to section 9.92 for additional details o f the system. 
     NOTE : In order to erase the Chaff and Flare d ispensing libraries, STBY 
            position is required on CFDCP.

2.7  Master Armament Safety Switch (M.A.S.S) 
     The M.A.S.S controls the power supply to the s ystem. This control is 
     equipped with a safety pin in order to secure,  before flight, systems 
     connected to this centralized power supply. 
     In the EWS installation, the M.A.S.S is comple ted by the CFSS located on 
     the CFDCP (Figure 12, item 4). 
                                  Figure 10

|  ITEM  |      DESCRIPTION          |                 FUNCTION              |

|   1    | ON/OFF switch             |- ON  : Autho rizes the firing          | 
|        |                           |        funct ion of the MDU            | 
|        |                           |- OFF : Inhib its firing                | 
|        |                           |        (CFD MASS safety systems       | 
|        |                           |         acti vated)                    | 
|   2    | Safety pin                |Locks the ON/ OFF switch in OFF position| 
|        |                           |to prevent an y untimely activation     | 
|        |                           |                                       | 
2.8  EWS audio control panel 
     EWS audio switch, installed just below the TWI , permits to the crew to  
     switch-off EWS audio alert but with a visual a lert label EWS/AUDIO  
     on PCB. 
     EWC audio warning and caution are adjustable o n ICS for the crew except  
     for co-pilot who can adjust to minimum value a udible.

2.9  Chaff anf flare dispenser magazines   
 
     One Chaff and Flare magazine is located inside  each one of the CFD 
     (Figure 1, items 4 and 5). 
     One Chaff and Flare dispenser is installed on either side of helicopter 
     tail boom. 
 
2.10 Safety Pin Unit (SPU) 
 
     The Safety Pin Unit (SPU) is installed on the bottom of the tail boom. 
     The function of the Safety Pin Unit is to disa ble the dispense signal to 
     the fire circuit. 
     The SPU controls the 28V firing supply in the dispensers by means of 
     one safety pin. 
     To authorized the dispensing it's mandatory to  remove the safety pin on 
     the SPU. 
 
2.11  Dispensing controls  
 
       Three types of manual firing are accessible to the crew : 
 
       - Preventive firing (figure 3, item 1) locat ed on the EWS control 
         panel, 
       - Survive firing located on the cyclic contr ol, 
       - Jettison, located on CFDCP. 
 
2.11.1  Preventive control 
 
        The preventive control dispenses the select ed countermeasures  
        manual program (1 to 15).The manual program  is selected via the 
        TWI (Refer to paragraph 2.4.3). 
        
2.11.2  Survive control 
 
        The survive pushbutton dispenses the counte rmeasures manual program 1.  
        The survive sequence is an emergency progra mmable sequence. It can be 
        activated by the crew (cyclic control, refe r to section 7.2 ) or cabin 
        passengers, if authorized.

COMPLEMENTARY FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 9.96
15-44 Page 27
*01* 
     . Survive command - Crew : 
       The survive command - crew is located on eac h cyclic control.  
       (Figure 13, item 1). 
                                    Figure 1 2
     . Survive command - Cabin passengers : 
       Cabin fire authorization  
       The cabin firing authorization switch author izes the cabin passengers 
       to dispenser cartridges. This switch is loca ted in the pedestal on the 
       EWS control panel (Figure 3, item 3). 
       Cabin firing localization 
       - There are 4 buttons located in the cabin. Once the cabin  
         authorization switch (figure 3, item 3) is  enabled and all safety 
         interlocks are removed (MASS, CFSS, SPU, i n flight, CFDCP mode other 
         than STBY), these buttons allow the dispen sing of chaff and flare  
         cartridges according to manual program 1.  
                                     Figure 1 3
2.11.3  Jettison command 
        The jettison control ensures the "JETTISON"  function which makes 
        it possible to eject the chaff and flare ca rtridges considered  
        dangerous in the event of an emergency land ing.  
        This sequence is available in all CFDCP mod es, except STBY.

1.1 Effect of MPAIs on level flight performance
NOTE The performance in level flight is not affected by the MPAI
bullet being open.
MPAIs with bullets closed
Figure 1 indicates the corrections in % to be applied to the torque
values with both engines running and 1 engine inoperative.
- Fuel consumption data must be increased by 1 %.

2 INDICATION OF PERFORMANCE DATA
For given altitude and temperature conditions, each chart indicates, on
iso-weight curves, the airspeed and fuel consumption data depending on
torque (two engines operative, one engine inoperative).
Select the altitude/temperature combination which is nearest to the
prevailing flight conditions; interpolate if necessary.
Maximum endurance speed
This is shown by the dotted line marked E on the iso-weight curves.
Best range speed
This is given inside the block, at the top of the figure.
MAKILA 1A2 engine Bleed Valve modification (TU 223)
R
POST TU 223, twin engine consumption rates are increased by 8%.The
flight phases affected by this increase are shown by shaded areas on
the Flight Manual charts (Figures 2b to 31b).
